We could orient each cell using the standard orientation of a disk
\mathbb{D}^{n}
, but then we have to choose a fundamental class in the singular cohomology
H^{n}(\mathbb{D}^{n},\partial\mathbb{D}^{n})
.

Thus to compute the cellular homology and cohomology of
\mathbb{L}_{p}^{\infty}
, it would be best for us to define a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-simplicial complex structure on
\mathbb{S}^{\infty}
. Do this inductively, modifying the construction in
[
22
, p. 145]
. Give
\mathbb{S}^{1}
the simplicial complex structure of a counterclockwise regular
p
-gon. That is, the vertices are the roots of unity
1,\zeta_{p},\dotsc,\zeta_{p}^{p-1}
, where
\zeta_{p}=e^{2\pi i/p}
, and the edges are
[\zeta_{p}^{j},\zeta_{p}^{j+1}]
where
1\leq j\leq p
. Inductively, assume that
\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}
has been given a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-simplicial complex structure. Then the structure on
\mathbb{S}^{2n+1}
is defined as follows: take the
\mathbb{S}^{1}
in the
(n+1)^{\text{th}}

\mathbb{C}
factor and give it the counter-clockwise regular
p
-gon simplicial complex structure. Then for any
k
-simplex
[v_{0},\dotsc,v_{k}]
in
\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}
, add
p

(k+1)
-simplices
[\zeta_{p}^{j},v_{0},\dotsc,v_{k}]
and
p

(k+2)
-simplices
[\zeta_{p}^{j},\zeta_{p}^{j+1}v_{0},\dotsc,v_{k}]
for
1\leq j\leq p
. This structure is a triangulation of each cell, and it is compatible with the (relative) fundamental classes of each cell taken to be simply the sum of the top-dimensional simplices in the cell that have been mentioned so far. In this way, the cellular boundary maps are really multiplication by
p
.

With orientations for each cell chosen in this way, let
\omega_{k}
denote the dual to the
k
-cell.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
n\geq 1
. Then thinking of
\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}
as the unit sphere in
(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})^{p}
, and considering the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
action on
(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})^{p}
by cyclic permutations,
(\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}/\tilde{\Delta})/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is homeomorphic to the Thom space
(L_{n}\times\mathbb{D}^{n+1})/(L_{n}\times\partial\mathbb{D}^{n+1})
, where
\tilde{\Delta}=\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}\cap\{(x,\dotsc,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{p(n+1)}~|~x\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\}
.

(Recall that
L_{n}
was defined in
eq.

1.3
.)

Proof.

Let us prove this for odd primes
p
as the case where
p=2
is similar. We will show that
\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}-\tilde{\Delta}
is homeomorphic to a trivial rank
n+1
real vector bundle over
\mathbb{L}_{p}((1,\dotsc,\frac{p-1}{2})^{n+1})
.

\mathbb{Z}_{p}
acts on
\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{p(n+1)}
by
M_{n+1}=M\otimes I_{n+1}
, where
M
is the
p\times p
circulant matrix. We have
M_{n+1}=F_{n+1}D_{n+1}F_{n+1}^{-1}
, where
F_{n+1}=F_{\mathbb{R}}\otimes I_{n+1}
and
D_{n+1}=D_{\mathbb{R}}\otimes I_{n+1}
. Since
F_{n+1}
is orthogonal,
F_{n+1}^{-1}
restricts to a homeomorphism
\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}\to\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{p(n+1)}
, where both the domain and target spheres are the unit sphere. Moreover,
F_{n+1}^{-1}
sends
\tilde{\Delta}
to
\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}\cap V
, where
V=\vspan\{v_{0m}\}_{m=1}^{n+1}
.

Note that
\dim V=n+1
and thus
\dim V^{\perp}=(p-1)(n+1)
. Since every vector subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{p(n+1)}
intersects transversally with
\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}
,
\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}\cap V
is homeomorphic to
\mathbb{S}^{n}
and
\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}\cap V^{\perp}
is homeomorphic to
\mathbb{S}^{(p-1)(n+1)-1}=\mathbb{S}^{pn-n+p-2}
.

Therefore there is a homeomorphism from
\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}
to the Thom space of the trivial rank
n+1
real bundle over
\mathbb{S}^{(p-1)(n+1)-1}
, which takes a point in
\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}
, expresses it uniquely as
u+v
for
u\in V^{\perp}
and
v\in V
, then outputs the basepoint if
\lVert v\rVert=1
, and outputs
(u/\lVert u\rVert,v)
otherwise.

\mathbb{Z}_{p}
acts via
D_{n+1}
on the target
\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}
, which is the identity on
V
and rotates each
W_{jm}
by angle
j\theta
, for
\theta=2\pi/p
. Hence if we write the coordinates of
V^{\perp}
as
(z_{jm})
, where
z_{jm}=x_{jm}+iy_{jm}
has real and imaginary parts being the real coordinates associated with the respective basis vectors
u_{jm}
and
w_{jm}
, then
D_{n+1}
acts on
\mathbb{S}^{(p-1)(n+1)-1}
by the map
z_{jm}\mapsto\zeta_{p}^{j}z_{jm}
; thus
\mathbb{S}^{(p-1)(n+1)-1}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\sim\mathbb{L}_{p}((1,\dotsc,\frac{p-1}{2})^{n+1})=L
.
D_{n+1}
also sends the fibers of the trivial complex bundle to each other while preserving the
v_{0m}
-coordinates, so the quotient of the bundle by the action of
D_{n+1}
is the trivial rank-
(n+1)
real vector bundle over
L
, which we can write as
L\times\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
.

Finally,
(\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}/\tilde{\Delta})/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is the one-point compactification of
(\mathbb{S}^{p(n+1)-1}-\tilde{\Delta})/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\sim L\times\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
, which is the Thom space of
L\times\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
.
∎
